# Mutashabihat tree rules audit

Audited **2026-11-16**, tree snapshot **`f9107641`**, rebased and validated on **`9dc633c2`**. The intervening main change affects qira’at data, its generator/tests and records only; all audited tree inputs and builders are unchanged. Report only; no app code, corpus, verdict, generated card or test file is changed.

## Counts

**20,500 tree configurations checked**: 12,558 branch presentations and 7,942 compare trees. This includes ordinary pages, direct links to stored subgroups, and the website's review view. A compare card with multiple partners or story rows produces more than one tree; each selectable partner and each row was checked.

| Rule | Violations, by node/path/title | Affected tree configurations | Ordinary / direct / review instances |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| 1. Empty or placeholder nodes | 1,024 | 1,024 | 0 / 0 / 1,024 |
| 2. Standalone amber words | 1,225 | 1,142 | 805 / 21 / 399 |
| 3. Mushaf signs | 62 | 62 | 42 / 0 / 20 |
| 4. Words exactly once, in order | 0 | 0 | 0 / 0 / 0 |
| 5. Titles fit every member | 15 | 15 | 2 / 13 / 0 |

Counts are occurrences of the rule, not independent implementation bugs. A tree can occur under several rules. The affected-tree lists below group repeated instances by card/group and list every affected row/partner or node. “Ordinary” means the normal unfiltered page; “direct” means a stored subgroup accessible by its own URL but absent from the deduplicated catalogue; “review” means `review=1`. Review findings are not presented as defects visible in the ordinary phone feed.

- Normal catalogue: **8,151** branch trees and **2,727** compare trees, on **1,397** listed compare pages.
- Additional stored/direct subgroup presentations: **2,576**. These cover stored occurrence patterns absent from the deduplicated catalogue.
- Additional review configurations: **1,831** branch trees and **5,215** compare trees. These include dropped groups/cards and review story rows.
- Skipped **1,221** compare cards marked `view: "matrix"`, before expanding partners or rows, and **one** matrix-only branch group because it has no tree.
- Traversed **102,117** builder nodes and reconstructed **66,538** verse/side paths. Empty structural roots and through-lines were distinguished from rendered boxes.
- Independently compared all **6,236** corpus verses with the bundled **604** mushaf pages through the existing font conversion. **Zero word-text mismatches** remained. Waqf/sajda presentation annotations were handled as the repo's own conversion handles them; letters and vowel distinctions were retained.

## Shared implementation and checks

The drawer is shared within each platform, but there are two shared model entry points. Branches use `phrasePresentation` / `createPhrasePresenter` followed by `foldForms`; compare uses `compareTree`, with `braidOf` / `foldStrands`, which imports the branch spelling rules. The latter supports rejoins. This distinction matters: using the same drawer does not prove that both builders enforce the same invariants.

- [shared/quran/phraseTrees.ts:163](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/phraseTrees.ts#L163)
- [shared/quran/foldForms.ts:27](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/foldForms.ts#L27)
- [shared/quran/braid.ts:71](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/braid.ts#L71)
- [shared/quran/braid.ts:174](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/braid.ts#L174)
- [shared/quran/oneTree.test.ts:29](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/oneTree.test.ts#L29)
- [shared/quran/phraseTrees.test.ts:252](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/phraseTrees.test.ts#L252)
- [shared/quran/braid.test.ts:48](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/braid.test.ts#L48)
- [shared/quran/treeLabels.test.ts:37](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/treeLabels.test.ts#L37)

The automated audit calls the production inventory and page-card functions, rather than inventing sample pairs. It covers the three catalogue kinds (`mutashabeh,pondering,words`), the stored tree and subtree occurrence patterns, public cards, review cards, every one-to-many option and every story row. For each verse it follows its own `forms`, prefix and continuation, including rejoins. Same-surah compare cards select forms by the actual verse keys, not merely by surah. That avoids mistaking the other verse's form for missing Quran text.

For phone/web parity, all 1,397 ordinary compare pages were compared against `shownCards`, the phone's decoder. After canonicalizing link identifiers, which have different names but the same relationships, all card sides, words, marks, options and story rows matched. The common builders therefore receive equivalent inputs for ordinary pages.

**Automated:** exhaustive builder traversal; visible-box classification from both drawers; single-word amber detection through `tones`, `differs`, `writtenForms`, and the renderer’s `treeContexts` / `likenessOf` result; complete word-path reconstruction; branch and compare end-sign positions/numbers, including the branch drawer’s final `shownAyat` / `endSign`; corpus/mushaf equivalence; title coverage against every presented member; phone/web input parity; React DOM spot checks.

**Checked by eye:** three local production website renders at 1440×1100, with reduced motion: the standalone amber موسى box in `cmp:08xcuew0p84ori`; the copied end sign in `2:116|18:4`; and the review-only empty-side note in `2:124-125|3:96-97`. Also read the seven named-family/title exceptions from the initial catalogue title scan and the additional stored-title failures. The remaining affected cases were verified programmatically, not individually opened in a browser. No phone screenshots were taken; phone rendering conclusions come from shared inputs and its `BranchTree`/`CompareTree` code.

This is exhaustive for the enumerated catalogue/stored/review configurations, not every possible user-created subset, juz filter, collapsed-row choice, surrounding-verse toggle, zoom or colour setting. Those state combinations are not included in the counts. Amber checks use the normal by-kind colour mode with no path pinned. It is a correctness audit, not a fresh scholarly review of which ayat should be grouped.

## Rule 1: no empty or placeholder nodes

**Finding R1-A. Empty story sides become explanatory placeholder boxes, instead of a line.** All 1,024 instances are in review configurations. The node has no Quran words and no terminal ayah, but `renderNode` supplies a rope and «لا تذكر سورة … هذا الجزء». Because the custom content exists, the drawer bypasses its through-line case and creates a bordered `PathBox`. This is a literal violation of the requested no-placeholder rule; the note is clearly explanatory UI, not a fabricated ayah. Severity: low. The behavior is checked in a website render and in both platform implementations; interpreting the explanatory box as forbidden follows the requested rule.

Root cause: [src/app/quran/compare/CompareTree.tsx:24](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/src/app/quran/compare/CompareTree.tsx#L24) and [mobile/src/components/CompareTree.tsx:35](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/mobile/src/components/CompareTree.tsx#L35) override the through-line condition in [src/app/quran/branches/BranchTree.tsx:159](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/src/app/quran/branches/BranchTree.tsx#L159) and [mobile/src/components/BranchTree.tsx:477](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/mobile/src/components/BranchTree.tsx#L477).

Suggested fix: keep the empty side as a continuous line. If the explanatory note is needed, place it outside the tree node. Keep the distinction between an invisible structural node and a visible empty box.

No empty word box, dot node or ellipsis marker was found on an ordinary tree. The scan encountered 7,934 empty structural nodes, including roots and through-lines; their mere presence in the model was not counted as a violation. A sign-only boundary drawn without a box was likewise not counted as an empty node.

## Rule 2: amber words never stand alone

**Finding R2-A. The folds can leave a one-word amber node, or create one in an empty prefix.** The branch fold moves only one boundary word per pass, protects a one-word trunk, and only moves down when each child accepts a single form. It can move a differently written first word into an otherwise empty prefix, creating a one-word amber box. A node left with another differing word is not necessarily folded again. In addition, `TreeWords` can colour a word amber through `treeContexts` / `likenessOf` even when the node has no `forms`, `differs` or `tones`; folding must cover that colour source too. Compare applies a similar boundary-only pass and a trunk-retention exception. These existing exceptions do not satisfy the stricter rule in this audit. Severity: medium. The standalone موسى example was checked visually; every listed node was checked from its builder output and the drawer's actual amber inputs.

Root causes: [shared/quran/foldForms.ts:31](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/foldForms.ts#L31) and [shared/quran/foldForms.ts:47](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/foldForms.ts#L47); compare uses [shared/quran/braid.ts:71](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/braid.ts#L71).

Suggested fix: enforce the invariant on the completed presentation, including prefixes, not just on one movement. Merge each amber-only one-word box into an adjacent word-bearing box for that path. Fold complete remaining runs or repeat the fold where necessary; if an inner shared box disappears, retain the continuous line/rejoin. Preserve each path's own spelling. Do not solve this by deleting or blending the word.

The representative ordinary branch URL is `cmp:08xcuew0p84ori`, refs 7:117, 10:87, 26:52, 26:63: a box contains only amber «موسى». The full affected list follows. A word in a larger box is not counted merely because its internal spelling braid has one word. A numeral after a single word does not make that box a multiword node.

`r` is the root, `.0`, `.1`, etc. are child indices, `>` follows a rejoin. “prefix” denotes a box before the trunk. These are audit locations, not new Quran markers.

## Rule 3: only correctly placed mushaf signs

**Finding R3-A. A shared compare node copies one side's verse-end sign into the other side's continuing verse.** There are 62 affected path/configuration instances, 42 on ordinary cards and 20 in additional review configurations. Severity: high for Quran annotation correctness. The word sequence remains intact; the error is an extra verse boundary/number on the other side's path. Every branch path was also checked for boundary positions and numbers, including continuations and the final sign appended by `shownAyat` / `endSign`; no additional branch-sign violations were found.

The concrete cause is [shared/quran/braid.ts:147](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/braid.ts#L147): `both` takes the union of `word.end` and `partner.end`, then pushes the resulting sign into the shared words and **both** forms. It does so even if only one side ends there.

For example, [al-Baqara 116 / al-Kahf 4](https://namaq.vercel.app/quran/compare?a=2&b=18#c-2-116-18-4) shares «اتخذ الله ولدا». The shared box shows the al-Kahf 4 medallion. Following al-Baqara's path therefore passes a “4” after its fourth word «ولدا», then continues «سبحانه…» before its real end at word 14. This was confirmed visually in the local production render.

Suggested fix: retain end markers per side/path. Only the ayah actually ending at that word may receive that sign. A path continuing through that point should remain a line, not acquire an empty/sign placeholder node. Where both verses end together with different numbers, the existing neutral medallion can still represent their shared end, with correct per-verse references.

The al-Naml 26 example is not reported again: the raw corpus token still contains a sajda suffix after «العظيم», but `mushafWords` maps that form to the word without the suffix and `mushafText` removes sajda annotation from fallback text. The rendered compare tree does not stick that sign to «العظيم». A corpus-only scan would falsely report the already-fixed case.

No new dot, ellipsis or non-mushaf placeholder token was found in Quran word nodes. UI reference separators and the explicitly identified explanatory notes are not classified as Quran punctuation.

In the table, positions are counts of corpus word tokens before an end sign. Actual versus expected boundaries are given for the affected side only; side 1/2 denotes the first/second displayed surah. An unnumbered `۝` is accepted when both verse ends share that location.

## Rule 4: every verse word exactly once, in order, in its own writing

**No word-path violation found in the 66,538 reconstructed paths.** Prefixes, the shared trunk, child branches, spelling forms, continuations and compare rejoins reconstruct their own source words once and in order. This result includes all selected partners, same-surah comparisons, and story rows. It does not excuse Rule 3's extra end signs, which were checked independently.

The independent source check reconstructed all 6,236 ayat from `shared/data/quran/mushaf/1.json` through `604.json`. It compared their letters and vowel marks with `trees.json` after the repo's `mushafText` conversion. The raw spellings are not naively compared across the two font encodings. The annotation exclusions are the ones in `scripts/quran/buildMushafWords.ts:33`, including the mushaf's sajda decoration. Both complete texts and per-builder source token order were checked; a bag-of-words equality or diacritic-stripped equality was not used as the proof.

## Rule 5: a group title fits every member

**Finding R5-A. Two catalogue titles promise a continuation absent from one displayed member.** Both include «يا أيها…», while the al-Ma'idah 34 path stops at «غفور رحيم» and does not include ayah 35. The other members can supply the continuation; that does not make it a correct title for every member. Checked against the full presentation, not just the original short occurrence span. Severity: medium.

Suggested fix: derive the heading from what every current path actually shares, or name the comparison without quoting a continuation not shown for all members. If the intended group includes the following verse, make that scope and its references explicit; do not silently change the label alone to imply a larger passage.

**Finding R5-B. Thirteen stored/direct subgroup titles contain the blended form «شَئًْا», written by none of their members.** This is a generated title spelling, not a failure of the verse paths: the paths retain their individual forms. The title bypasses `writtenForms` and is rendered as ordinary Quran text in search/list labels. The unusual stacked vowel/sukun and missing letter are not justified by any member's Uthmani form. Severity: medium. These are additional direct-link groups, not ordinary catalogue entries.

Suggested fix: build a title from a real member's writing and make its representative role explicit, or use a neutral common title. Do not expose `sharedDisplay`'s synthetic common spelling as though it were a quoted Quran word. Preserve the per-ayah forms in the tree.

Title generation/data flow: [shared/quran/phraseTrees.ts:86](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/phraseTrees.ts#L86), `shared/data/quran/trees.json` / its subtree labels, [shared/quran/subtrees.ts:48](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/shared/quran/subtrees.ts#L48), and [src/app/quran/branches/OpeningSearch.tsx:20](https://github.com/msskzx/namaq/blob/f9107641/src/app/quran/branches/OpeningSearch.tsx#L20).

The automated title screen checked each title against each full displayed path, allowing the existing matching rules for vowel/hamza forms and an attached initial conjunction. Five non-quotation family names were reviewed separately against their enumerated membership: الطواسين، الراءات، الحامدات، المسبحات، and الم / المص / المر. They are collective labels, not strings required to appear verbatim in every verse, and were not reported as failures. A representative wording that differs only in vocalization or an attached conjunction was likewise not treated as a bad semantic title.

All 15 affected groups follow. The refs column lists every member; “fails” identifies the members that cannot supply the title's words in their displayed path.

## Suggested fix order and validation

1. Correct the per-side verse boundaries in `both` first. This places another ayah's end number inside Quran text on ordinary cards.
2. Enforce the completed-tree one-word amber invariant across branch prefixes, branch nodes and compare nodes. Do not fix only one drawer or one page.
3. Repair the 15 headings using genuine per-member text and correct scope.
4. Move review-only silent-side notes outside bordered Quran nodes, leaving a through-line.

Independent review identified two gaps in the initial audit harness: renderer-derived amber colours and branch end-sign checks. Both were added, the full scan was rerun, and all additional cases were included in the counts and tables. The branch-sign check found no further violations.

The existing `oneTree.test.ts` checks source structure and imports. It does not run every card or prove the five behavioral invariants. Preserve those structural checks, and add production-data assertions for side-specific end positions and final visible one-word amber boxes. A regression test for the two overlong headings should compare against the final presentation rather than just the cached occurrence range.

Validation on this revision: `npm run lint`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, and **438 test files / 4,569 tests passed**. `npm run build` passed with the existing local preview Quran database URL. The first build lacked `DATABASE_URL`; supplying the already-seeded local database corrected the environment without changing a file. An additional temporary React DOM test rendered the three representative cases successfully. The full app UI suite was not rerun because this PR changes only a report. The three website renders above were checked separately with Playwright. No code or data fixes were applied.

Records used: R0020 kept every verse-ending path, including internal endings; R0026 and R0044 kept label/annotation claims scoped to the actual words shown; R0051 required auditing both builders, not merely seeing one drawer. No new record or helper file is added, to keep this PR to the requested report.

## Reproducing the audit without changing app code

The following read-only harnesses were run from the repo root with `npx tsx --tsconfig tsconfig.json`. Save the blocks under `/tmp` with the filenames shown and run them in order. Imports use this repo's aliases. They write results only under `/tmp`. Run on the audited revision before comparing counts; later fixes should change the results. The first harness also normalizes the phone decoder's link identifiers to check input parity. The second uses the actual card/tree builders and produces the findings above. The final independent check reads the bundled mushaf pages. The browser/React spot checks were separate from these exhaustive counts.

<details>
<summary>/tmp/tree-audit-probe.ts</summary>

```ts
import corpus from '@shared/data/quran/trees.json';
import generated from '@shared/data/quran/compareCards';
import {branchInventory} from '@shared/quran/branchInventory';
import {pairCards,listedPairs,reviewPairs} from '@/app/quran/compare/pairCards';
import {ALL_KINDS} from '@shared/quran/branchSearch';
import {compareMatrixOnly} from '@shared/quran/compareViews';
import {matrixOnly} from '@shared/quran/branchVerdicts';
import {shownCards} from '@shared/quran/compareCards';
import {subtreeIndex} from '@shared/quran/subtrees';
import {phraseTreeId} from '@shared/quran/mutashabih/identity';
import {droppedBranch} from '@shared/quran/branchVerdicts';
import {writeFileSync} from 'node:fs';
console.log('kinds',ALL_KINDS,'raw',corpus.trees.length);
const pieces=branchInventory({categories:ALL_KINDS},null,true);console.log('inventory',pieces.length,'matrix',pieces.filter(p=>matrixOnly(p)).length);
let cards:any[]=[];for(const p of listedPairs) cards.push(...pairCards(p.a,p.b).map(c=>({...c,page:p})));
console.log('pages',listedPairs.length,'cards',cards.length,'matrix',cards.filter(c=>compareMatrixOnly(c)).length,'many',cards.filter(c=>c.many).length,'rows',cards.filter(c=>c.sides.some((s:any)=>s.rows)).length);
const stable=(v:any):string=>JSON.stringify(v,(_k,x)=>x&&typeof x==='object'&&!Array.isArray(x)?Object.fromEntries(Object.keys(x).sort().map(k=>[k,x[k]])):x);
const occ=(g:any)=>stable(g.occurrences.map((o:any)=>[o.key,o.start,o.end]).sort());
const signatures=new Set(pieces.map(p=>occ(p.phrase)));let legacy=0;
for(const g of [...corpus.trees,...subtreeIndex(corpus.trees as any,corpus.subtrees as any).groups]){const sig=occ(g);if(signatures.has(sig))continue;signatures.add(sig);legacy++;const id=phraseTreeId(g);pieces.push({id,legacyId:g.id,keys:[...new Set(g.occurrences.map(o=>o.key))],phrase:g,opening:{id,text:g.text,count:new Set(g.occurrences.map(o=>o.key)).size},text:g.text,count:new Set(g.occurrences.map(o=>o.key)).size,legacy:true} as any);}
console.log('extra stored direct groups',legacy);
const projection=(c:any)=>{const links=new Map<string,string>();const ayah=(a:any)=>({number:a.number,words:a.words,marks:Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(a.marks??{}).map(([i,m]:any)=>{if(m.link&&!links.has(m.link))links.set(m.link,String(links.size));return[i,{kind:m.kind,...(m.link?{link:links.get(m.link)}:{})}];}))});return{id:c.id,sides:c.sides.map((s:any)=>({surah:s.surah,ayah:s.ayah,ayat:s.ayat.map(ayah),rows:s.rows??null})),many:c.many?{side:c.many.side,options:c.many.options.map((o:any)=>({id:o.id,ayah:ayah(o.ayah),shared:ayah(o.shared)}))}:null};};
const parity:any[]=[];const wordMap=new Map(corpus.ayat.map(a=>[a.key,a.words]));
for(const p of listedPairs){const phone=shownCards(generated as any,k=>wordMap.get(k),p.a,p.b);const web=pairCards(p.a,p.b);if(stable(phone.map(projection))!==stable(web.map(projection)))parity.push({page:p,phone,web});}
const seen=new Set(cards.map(c=>stable([c.page.a,c.page.b,projection(c)])));
let reviewExtra=0;for(const p of reviewPairs)for(const c of pairCards(p.a,p.b,true)){const sig=stable([p.a,p.b,projection(c)]);if(seen.has(sig))continue;seen.add(sig);reviewExtra++;cards.push({...c,page:p,review:true});}
console.log('review extra',reviewExtra,'parity',parity.length,'branches dropped',pieces.filter(p=>droppedBranch(p)).length);
writeFileSync('/tmp/tree-audit-parity.json',JSON.stringify(parity));
writeFileSync('/tmp/tree-audit-input.json',JSON.stringify({pieces:pieces.map(p=>({...p,review:droppedBranch(p)})),cards}));
```

<summary>/tmp/tree-rules-scan.ts</summary>

```ts
import fs from 'node:fs';
import corpus from '@shared/data/quran/trees.json';
import {branchInventory} from '@shared/quran/branchInventory';
import {ALL_KINDS} from '@shared/quran/branchSearch';
import {phrasePresentation,letters} from '@shared/quran/phraseTrees';
import {groupPresentation} from '@shared/quran/mutashabih/presentation';
import {compareTree,sideKey} from '@shared/quran/braid';
import {writtenLinks} from '@shared/quran/compareCardLook';
import {compareMatrixOnly} from '@shared/quran/compareViews';
import {matrixOnly} from '@shared/quran/branchVerdicts';
import {keys,branchesHref,shownAyat,endSign} from '@shared/quran/branches';
import {mushafText} from '@shared/quran/mushafText';
import {compareAnchor} from '@shared/compareBookmarks';
import {treeContexts,likenessOf} from '@shared/quran/ayahContext';
import {writtenForms} from '@shared/quran/writtenForms';
import type {Branch,BranchAyah} from '@shared/quran/branches';
import type {CardSide} from '@shared/quran/compareCards';
const all=corpus.ayat as BranchAyah[],byKey=new Map(all.map(a=>[a.key,a])),idx=new Map(all.map((a,i)=>[a.key,i]));
const input=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync('/tmp/tree-audit-input.json','utf8'));
const end=(w:string)=>w==='۝'||/^﴿[٠-٩]+﴾$/.test(w);
const raw=(ws:string[])=>ws.filter(w=>!end(w));
const normalized=(ws:string[])=>raw(ws).map(w=>mushafText(w).replace(/[\s\u200e\u200f\u2060]/g,'').normalize('NFC'));
const lexical=(ws:string[])=>raw(ws).filter(w=>letters(w).length>0);
const eq=(a:any,b:any)=>JSON.stringify(a)===JSON.stringify(b);
const issues:any[]=[];const titleCandidates:any[]=[];const counts:any={branchTrees:0,branchSkippedMatrix:0,compareCards:0,compareTrees:0,compareSkippedMatrix:0,nodes:0,paths:0,structuralEmpty:0};
let context:any;let paintAyat:BranchAyah[]=[];let paintOffsets:Record<string,number>={};let painted:ReturnType<typeof treeContexts>|undefined;
function paintedAmber(node:any,i:number,pathLength:number,prefix=false,ownKeys=keys(node)){
 if(context.type!=='branches')return false;
 painted??=treeContexts(paintAyat);
 const matches=ownKeys.map(key=>likenessOf(painted!.find(c=>c.ayah.key===key)?.match,(prefix?0:paintOffsets[key]??0)+pathLength+i));
 const kind=node.tones?node.tones[i]:matches.length&&matches.every(Boolean)?matches.includes('root')?'root':matches.includes('written')?'written':'exact':undefined;
 const f=writtenForms(node,i,node.words[i]);return kind==='written'||(f[0].word!==node.words[i]&&kind!=='root');
}
function issue(rule:number,kind:string,detail:any){issues.push({...context,rule,kind,...detail});}
function visit(node:Branch,root=false,path='r',silent=false,pathLength=0){
 counts.nodes++;const words=lexical(node.words);
 if(!words.length){
  if(!node.words.length&&!node.ayat.length){counts.structuralEmpty++;if(silent)issue(1,'silent-note',{node:path,words:node.words});}
  else if(node.ayat.length)issue(1,'empty-visible',{node:path,words:node.words,keys:keys(node)});
 }
 const at=node.words.map((w,i)=>!end(w)&&letters(w).length?i:-1).filter(i=>i>=0);
 if(words.length===1){
  const i=at[0];const forms=writtenForms(node,i,node.words[i]);
  if(node.tones?.[i]==='written'||node.differs?.includes(i)||forms.length>1||paintedAmber(node,i,pathLength))issue(2,'lone-amber',{node:path,words:node.words,forms:node.forms,tones:node.tones,keys:keys(node)});
 }
 const displayed=[...(node.forms?Object.values(node.forms).flat():node.words)].map(mushafText);
 if(displayed.some(w=>/[⋯…·]|\.{2,}|[□◌]/u.test(w)))issue(1,'placeholder',{node:path,words:displayed});
 node.children.forEach((c,i)=>visit(c,false,`${path}.${i}`,silent,pathLength+node.words.length));if(node.rejoin)visit(node.rejoin,false,`${path}>`,false,pathLength+node.words.length);
}
function pathWords(node:Branch,key:string,parents:string[]=[]):{words:string[],last:Branch,parents:string[]}|undefined{
 if(node.ayat.includes(key))return{words:node.forms?.[key]??node.words,last:node,parents};
 for(const c of node.children){const p=pathWords(c,key,[...parents,...node.words]);if(p)return{words:[...(node.forms?.[key]??node.words),...p.words],last:p.last,parents:p.parents};}
}
function expected(first:string,last:string){
 const from=idx.get(first)!,to=idx.get(last)!;const out:string[]=[];
 if(to<from||to-from>1000)return[];
 for(let i=from;i<=to;i++){const a=all[i];if(i>from&&a.key.endsWith(':1')&&!a.key.startsWith('9:'))out.push(...byKey.get('1:1')!.words);out.push(...a.words);}
 return out;
}
for(const piece of input.pieces){
 if(matrixOnly(piece)){counts.branchSkippedMatrix++;continue;}
 context={type:'branches',id:piece.id,title:piece.text,url:'https://namaq.vercel.app'+branchesHref(piece.id,undefined,{filters:{categories:ALL_KINDS}}),refs:piece.keys,review:piece.review,legacy:!!piece.legacy};
 let p;try{p=piece.group?groupPresentation(piece.group,byKey):phrasePresentation(piece.phrase,all);}catch(e){issue(4,'builder-throws',{error:String(e)});continue;}
 counts.branchTrees++;paintAyat=p.ayat;paintOffsets=p.offsets;painted=undefined;visit(p.tree,true);
 for(const pre of p.prefixes){const ws=pre.words??(pre.word?pre.word.split(' '):[]);if(lexical(ws).length===1&&((pre.differs?.length??0)>0||new Set(Object.values(pre.forms??{}).map(w=>w.join(' '))).size>1||paintedAmber({...pre,words:ws},ws.findIndex(w=>letters(w).length>0),0,true,pre.keys)))issue(2,'lone-amber-prefix',{words:ws,keys:pre.keys});}
 for(const key of piece.keys){counts.paths++;const path=pathWords(p.tree,key);const prefix=p.prefixes.find(v=>v.keys.includes(key));if(!path){issue(4,'missing-path',{key});continue;}
  const words=[...(prefix?.forms?.[key]??prefix?.words??(prefix?.word?prefix.word.split(' '):[])),...path.words];
  const final=path.last.through?.[key]??key;const want=expected(key,final);
  if(!eq(normalized(words),normalized(want)))issue(4,'branch-path-mismatch',{key,final,got:words,want,displayGot:normalized(words),displayWant:normalized(want)});
  else if(!eq(raw(words),want))issue(0,'font-equivalent',{key});
  const rendered=[...words];if(!end(path.last.words.at(-1)??'')){const shown=shownAyat(path.last,path.parents);rendered.push(...endSign(path.last.ayat.map(k=>shown[k].at(-1)!)));}
  const actual=ends(rendered);let at=0;const desired:any[]=[];for(let n=idx.get(key)!;n<=idx.get(final)!;n++){const a=all[n];if(n>idx.get(key)!&&a.key.endsWith(':1')&&!a.key.startsWith('9:'))at+=byKey.get('1:1')!.words.length;at+=a.words.length;desired.push({at,number:+a.key.split(':')[1]});}
  if(!eq(actual.map(e=>e.at),desired.map(e=>e.at)))issue(3,'branch-end-position',{key,final,actual,desired,got:rendered});
  else if(actual.some((e,i)=>e.sign!=='۝'&&e.sign!==`﴿${String(desired[i].number).replace(/\d/g,d=>'٠١٢٣٤٥٦٧٨٩'[+d])}﴾`))issue(3,'branch-end-number',{key,final,actual,desired});
 }
 const title=piece.text.split(' ').map(letters);const mismatches=piece.phrase.occurrences.filter((o:any)=>!eq(all[idx.get(o.key)!].words.slice(o.start,o.end).map(letters),title));
 if(mismatches.length){const titleWords=piece.text.split(' ').filter(w=>!end(w)).map(w=>letters(w));const misses=p.ayat.flatMap(a=>{const ws=a.words.filter(w=>!end(w)).map(w=>letters(w));let at=0;for(const w of ws)if(titleWords[at]!==undefined&&[w,w.replace(/^[وف](?=..)/,'')].some(v=>[titleWords[at],titleWords[at].replace(/^[وف](?=..)/,'')].includes(v)))at++;return at<titleWords.length?[{key:a.key,matched:at,total:titleWords.length,words:a.words}]:[];});if(misses.length)titleCandidates.push({...context,occurrences:piece.phrase.occurrences,misses});}
}
function comparePath(node:Branch,owner:Map<Branch,0|1>,side:0|1,allowed:Set<string>):string[]{
 const own=owner.get(node);if(own!==undefined&&own!==side)return[];
 const form=Object.entries(node.forms??{}).find(([k])=>allowed.has(k))?.[1];
 return [...(form??node.words),...node.children.flatMap(c=>comparePath(c,owner,side,allowed)),...(node.rejoin?comparePath(node.rejoin,owner,side,allowed):[])];
}
function ends(ws:string[]){let at=0;const out:any[]=[];for(const w of ws){if(end(w))out.push({at,sign:w});else at++;}return out;}
for(const card of input.cards){
 if(compareMatrixOnly(card)){counts.compareSkippedMatrix++;continue;}
 counts.compareCards++;const opts=card.many?card.many.options.map((_:any,i:number)=>i):[0];
 for(const option of opts){
  const selected=card.many?.options[option];const sides=card.sides.map((s:any,i:number)=>!selected?s:i===card.many.side?{...s,ayah:selected.ayah.number,ayat:[selected.ayah]}:{...s,ayat:[selected.shared]});
  const rows=sides[0].rows?sides[0].rows.map((_:any,row:number)=>sides.map((s:any)=>{const ayat=s.ayat.filter((a:any)=>s.rows[row].includes(a.number));return{...s,ayah:ayat[0]?.number??s.ayah,ayat,rows:undefined};})):[sides];
  for(let row=0;row<rows.length;row++){
   const ss=rows[row] as [CardSide,CardSide];context={type:'compare',id:card.id,review:!!card.review,url:`https://namaq.vercel.app/quran/compare?a=${card.page.a}&b=${card.page.b}#${compareAnchor(card.sides[0],card.sides[1])}`,option,row,refs:ss.flatMap(s=>s.ayat.map(a=>`${s.surah}:${a.number}`))};
   const built=compareTree(ss,writtenLinks({sides:ss}));counts.compareTrees++;
   const check=(n:Branch,root=false,path='r')=>{const owner=built.sides.get(n);const silent=owner!==undefined&&!n.words.length&&!ss[owner].ayat.length;const save=n.children,join=n.rejoin;n.children=[];n.rejoin=undefined;visit(n,root,path,silent);n.children=save;n.rejoin=join;save.forEach((c,i)=>check(c,false,`${path}.${i}`));if(join)check(join,false,`${path}>`);};check(built.tree,true);
   for(const side of [0,1] as const){counts.paths++;const got=comparePath(built.tree,built.sides,side,new Set(ss[side].ayat.map(a=>`${ss[side].surah}:${a.number}`)));const want=ss[side].ayat.flatMap(a=>a.words);
    if(!eq(normalized(got),normalized(want)))issue(4,'compare-path-mismatch',{side,got,want,displayGot:normalized(got),displayWant:normalized(want)});
    const actual=ends(got);let at=0;const desired=ss[side].ayat.map(a=>{at+=a.words.length;return{at,number:a.number};});
    if(!eq(actual.map(e=>e.at),desired.map(e=>e.at)))issue(3,'compare-end-position',{side,actual,desired,got});
    else if(actual.some((e,i)=>e.sign!=='۝'&&e.sign!==`﴿${String(desired[i].number).replace(/\d/g,d=>'٠١٢٣٤٥٦٧٨٩'[+d])}﴾`))issue(3,'compare-end-number',{side,actual,desired});
   }
  }
 }
}
const output={revision:'f9107641',counts,issues,titleCandidates};fs.writeFileSync('/tmp/tree-rules-results.json',JSON.stringify(output));
console.log(counts);console.log('issues',Object.fromEntries([...new Set(issues.map(i=>`${i.rule}:${i.kind}`))].map(k=>[k,issues.filter(i=>`${i.rule}:${i.kind}`===k).length])),'titleCandidates',titleCandidates.length);
```

<details>
<summary>/tmp/tree-canonical-check.ts</summary>

```ts
import fs from 'node:fs';
import corpus from '@shared/data/quran/trees.json';
import {mushafText} from '@shared/quran/mushafText';
let surah=0,words:string[]=[];const byKey=new Map<string,string[]>();
for(let page=1;page<=604;page++){const data=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(`shared/data/quran/mushaf/${page}.json`,'utf8'));for(const item of data.items){if(item.surah){surah=item.surah;words=[];}if(item.line)for(const token of item.line){if(typeof token==='number'){byKey.set(`${surah}:${token}`,words);words=[];}else if(token!=='۞')words.push(token);}}}
const raw=(t:string)=>t.replace(/[ۖ-ۜ۞ۤ۩]/g,'').replace(/[\s\u200e\u200f\u2060]/g,'').normalize('NFC');
const mismatches=corpus.ayat.flatMap(a=>raw(mushafText(a.words.join(' ')))!==raw((byKey.get(a.key)??[]).join(' '))?[{key:a.key,tree:a.words,mushaf:byKey.get(a.key),treeShown:raw(mushafText(a.words.join(' '))),mushafShown:raw((byKey.get(a.key)??[]).join(' '))}]:[]);
fs.writeFileSync('/tmp/tree-canonical-check.json',JSON.stringify({verses:byKey.size,mismatches}));console.log('mushaf verses',byKey.size,'mismatches',mismatches.length);console.log(mismatches.slice(0,2));
```

```sh
npx tsx --tsconfig tsconfig.json /tmp/tree-audit-probe.ts
npx tsx --tsconfig tsconfig.json /tmp/tree-rules-scan.ts
npx tsx --tsconfig tsconfig.json /tmp/tree-canonical-check.ts
```

The second harness's rule `0` observations, if any, are font-equivalent raw encodings and are not violations. Its `titleCandidates` include the five collective family names; the manual disposition above is required before counting title violations. Its `silent-note` observations are the literal no-placeholder policy findings, not claims that explanatory text is Quran text. The report groups repeated causes once and preserves the affected targets for follow-up fixes.
